# Skills · H · part 42

783 entries, hoc-code-review to holistic-app-build. [Index](README.md) · [Everything](../README.md)

- **hoc-code-review**: Run a READ-ONLY, code-level review of a change and produce a findings report. Never fixes anything. Use this skill whenever the user asks to review a change or pull request before it is merged, or to verify that what was built matches what was specified. Writing the requirement document it checks against belongs to the requirement definition convention. ([openreachtech/hora-skills-ort-core](https://github.com/openreachtech/hora-skills-ort-core/tree/HEAD/kit/skills/hoc-code-review) · ★17)
- **hoc-coding-styles**: Coding style conventions for how code is laid out across lines, and the forms literals are written in. Use when writing or reviewing the layout of any expression or statement. Which statements may be written belongs to the statements convention; the layout of a JSDoc block, to the JSDoc convention. ([openreachtech/hora-skills-ort-core](https://github.com/openreachtech/hora-skills-ort-core/tree/HEAD/kit/skills/hoc-coding-styles) · ★17)
- **hoc-collect-ctors**: How a set of classes, one per member, is collected into the one place callers ask: a barrel file, or a folder loaded once at startup, chosen by how an addition reaches its users. Use when variants are modelled as classes, when about to list them in an array or a keyed object, or when adding a barrel or a directory loader. Why a member is a class belongs to the class design principles convention. ([openreachtech/hora-skills-ort-core](https://github.com/openreachtech/hora-skills-ort-core/tree/HEAD/kit/skills/hoc-collect-ctors) · ★17)
- **hoc-comments**: Comment-writing conventions — what a comment says (the why, never a restatement, no annotation but `NOTE:`), where an inline comment stands (above the code, at a line end only to keep a format), where a long comment breaks (at a clause, never at a column), the form of a multi-line block, and lines commented out with `//`. Use when writing or reviewing any comment, or when commenting lines out. Which language a comment is written in belongs to the writing-language convention. ([openreachtech/hora-skills-ort-core](https://github.com/openreachtech/hora-skills-ort-core/tree/HEAD/kit/skills/hoc-comments) · ★17)
- **hoc-constants**: Conventions for constants — how they are named and written, and where the files holding them live. Use when declaring a constant or an enum-like object, or when deciding where one belongs. Names of everything else belong to the naming convention. ([openreachtech/hora-skills-ort-core](https://github.com/openreachtech/hora-skills-ort-core/tree/HEAD/kit/skills/hoc-constants) · ★17)
- **hoc-contracts**: Conventions for the type contracts of function and method arguments and return values, including how contract types are defined. ([openreachtech/hora-skills-ort-core](https://github.com/openreachtech/hora-skills-ort-core/tree/HEAD/kit/skills/hoc-contracts) · ★17)
- **hoc-dai-hoc-hieu-qua**: Cố vấn năng lực học đại học chung (Lớp A): giải mã đề cương học phần (syllabus & CLO), lập kế hoạch học tín chỉ theo TT 08/2021/TT-BGDĐT, phương pháp đọc giáo trình–ghi chép Cornell–Active Recall–Spaced Repetition, kỹ năng seminar/đồ án nhóm và kỷ luật sử dụng AI trung thực. ([Wothing0406/EduSkills-VN](https://github.com/Wothing0406/EduSkills-VN/tree/HEAD/skills/hoc-dai-hoc-hieu-qua) · ★2)
- **hoc-dependency-defect**: How to deal with a bug in code this project uses but does not own — a framework, an in-house package, anything inside `node_modules/`. Use when a package behaves wrongly and the correction has to live in this project. A bug in code this project owns is fixed directly instead. ([openreachtech/hora-skills-ort-core](https://github.com/openreachtech/hora-skills-ort-core/tree/HEAD/kit/skills/hoc-dependency-defect) · ★17)
- **hoc-deployment-document**: Write a deployment runbook through conversation with whoever will run it, for a server you manage, a managed container service or a serverless function. Use when the user asks for deployment steps, a release procedure or a hosting setup document. Executing the deployment and building the CI/CD pipeline are out of scope. ([openreachtech/hora-skills-ort-core](https://github.com/openreachtech/hora-skills-ort-core/tree/HEAD/kit/skills/hoc-deployment-document) · ★17)
- **hoc-documentation**: Documentation writing conventions for READMEs, design documents and any other prose a project carries. Use when writing or updating a document, and when a change moves a fact a document states. The README's own structure belongs to the README convention; which language a document is written in, to the writing-language convention. ([openreachtech/hora-skills-ort-core](https://github.com/openreachtech/hora-skills-ort-core/tree/HEAD/kit/skills/hoc-documentation) · ★17)
- **hoc-error-codes**: The string an error carries so a caller can tell it apart from every other, in the form `Aaa.XBBB.CCC`. Use when adding an error, or when choosing a category for a new failure. Declaring and throwing the code belong to each stack's own convention; whether a failure throws at all belongs to the errors convention. ([openreachtech/hora-skills-ort-core](https://github.com/openreachtech/hora-skills-ort-core/tree/HEAD/kit/skills/hoc-error-codes) · ★17)
- **hoc-errors**: Conventions for how code reports a failure, from a method that cannot produce its value to an abstract member a subclass has not implemented. Use when a method can fail, when the failure of a call to a delegate is turned into a result, or when declaring an abstract member. The string an error carries for its callers belongs to the error-codes convention; how the call to a delegate is laid out, to the delegation convention. ([openreachtech/hora-skills-ort-core](https://github.com/openreachtech/hora-skills-ort-core/tree/HEAD/kit/skills/hoc-errors) · ★17)
- **hoc-eslint-config**: Conventions and procedure for `eslint.config.js` and the shared config package it spreads. Use when raising that package, changing a rule or its options, reading what a rule reported, reaching for a disabling comment, or weighing a relaxation against the code change that would clear the rule. Scope is settled by the package name carrying `eslint`. Dependencies in general belong to the npm conventions. ([openreachtech/hora-skills-ort-core](https://github.com/openreachtech/hora-skills-ort-core/tree/HEAD/kit/skills/hoc-eslint-config) · ★17)
- **hoc-functions**: Conventions for functions. Use when defining a function. Its parameters follow the rule for method parameters, which belongs to the methods convention. ([openreachtech/hora-skills-ort-core](https://github.com/openreachtech/hora-skills-ort-core/tree/HEAD/kit/skills/hoc-functions) · ★17)
- **hoc-game-audio**: Generate, source, master, audition, and integrate Heroes of Crypto sound effects for unit attacks, damage, healing, resurrection, placement, and spellbook UI. Use for new or replacement effects, provided recordings, silence trimming, consistent loudness, compression, or playback fixes. Excludes music, spoken dialogue, and creature artwork. ([o1dstaRs/heroes-of-crypto-client](https://github.com/o1dstaRs/heroes-of-crypto-client/tree/HEAD/.claude/skills/hoc-game-audio) · ★3)
- **hoc-git-branch**: Conventions for the branches a repository carries — trunks, general branches, and the commits that open and merge them. Use before cutting a branch, merging one back, carrying a change onto one, or discarding one, and before the first edit or commit while `main`, `release/x.x.x`, `dev` or `env` is checked out. What a commit holds belongs to the git commit convention; pushing, to the git push convention. ([openreachtech/hora-skills-ort-core](https://github.com/openreachtech/hora-skills-ort-core/tree/HEAD/kit/skills/hoc-git-branch) · ★17)
- **hoc-git-branch-remote**: The branches a repository keeps on its remote: `main` as the default branch, which branches may merge into `main` by kind of repository — npm package, boilerplate or application — and why a trunk's published history is never rewritten. Use before opening a trunk, a pull request into `main`, or a rewrite or force-push of a trunk, or when deciding whether `env`, `hotfix/xxxx` or `dev` may be used. How branches are cut and merged belongs to the git branch convention. ([openreachtech/hora-skills-ort-core](https://github.com/openreachtech/hora-skills-ort-core/tree/HEAD/kit/skills/hoc-git-branch-remote) · ★17)
- **hoc-git-commit**: Conventions for git commits, in either message format. Use before writing a commit message, and before splitting a working tree into commits. The order commits land in belongs to the commit order convention; the branches commits land on, and the two commits a branch makes about itself, to the git branch convention; the commands that gate a commit, to the workflows convention. ([openreachtech/hora-skills-ort-core](https://github.com/openreachtech/hora-skills-ort-core/tree/HEAD/kit/skills/hoc-git-commit) · ★17)
- **hoc-git-commit-order**: The order commits land in once the work has been split: which of a test and the change it checks goes first, and how the rest of a branch is sequenced. Use when deciding which commit comes first, when tests and implementation change together, or when something is removed or a limit is lowered. What belongs in a single commit belongs to the git commit convention; which test cases fail against the old code, to the Jest convention. ([openreachtech/hora-skills-ort-core](https://github.com/openreachtech/hora-skills-ort-core/tree/HEAD/kit/skills/hoc-git-commit-order) · ★17)
- **hoc-git-prohibit-destructive-commands**: The git commands this library does not run, because what they destroy cannot be given back — a modified file, an untracked note, an object no ref still names — and what is reached for instead. Use before any command that would clear the working tree, discard a change or delete an object, and whenever something else needs the tree clean before it can run. Destroying what the remote holds belongs to the git push convention. ([openreachtech/hora-skills-ort-core](https://github.com/openreachtech/hora-skills-ort-core/tree/HEAD/kit/skills/hoc-git-prohibit-destructive-commands) · ★17)
- **hoc-git-push**: Conventions for `git push` itself, and for any command that changes what the remote holds. Use before every push, and before deleting a remote branch or tag. What a branch is and where it merges belongs to the git branch convention; what a pull request says belongs to the pull request convention. ([openreachtech/hora-skills-ort-core](https://github.com/openreachtech/hora-skills-ort-core/tree/HEAD/kit/skills/hoc-git-push) · ★17)
- **hoc-higher-order-functions**: How higher-order functions such as `map()`, `filter()`, `reduce()` and `forEach()` are used: what becomes of their return value, what a callback holds and is named, how elements are treated, and what `reduce()` may not be bent into. Use when writing or reviewing a callback passed to an array method, or when about to reach an element by index. Loops themselves belong to the statements convention; a collection a property holds, to the property-definition convention. ([openreachtech/hora-skills-ort-core](https://github.com/openreachtech/hora-skills-ort-core/tree/HEAD/kit/skills/hoc-higher-order-functions) · ★17)
- **hoc-idle-animation**: Create identity-preserving high-resolution standing idle loops for Heroes of Crypto creatures from canonical art and an optional 256x256 Sprite AI motion draft. Use for idle, standing, breathing, weight-shift, hover-in-place, or subtle ambient creature animation; do not use for walking, attacks, reactions, death, or static portraits. ([o1dstaRs/heroes-of-crypto-client](https://github.com/o1dstaRs/heroes-of-crypto-client/tree/HEAD/.claude/skills/hoc-idle-animation) · ★3)
- **hoc-implementation-progress**: Make the state of an in-flight implementation visible, in a progress document anchored to the requirement ids of the feature being built. Use this skill whenever implementing a defined feature spans more than one work item or session, or whenever the user asks how far along the work is or what is blocked. Defining the requirements belongs to the requirement definition convention. ([openreachtech/hora-skills-ort-core](https://github.com/openreachtech/hora-skills-ort-core/tree/HEAD/kit/skills/hoc-implementation-progress) · ★17)
- **hoc-jest**: Write Jest unit tests for JavaScript classes, and for the modules, data files and reconciliations a project tests alongside them. Use whenever a test is created or updated — for a class, a module of exported constants, a data file such as a message catalogue, or a reconciliation between two collections that have to agree. Driving a failing suite to green belongs to the test-execution convention, and reusing a recorded pass over unchanged inputs to the test-cache convention. ([openreachtech/hora-skills-ort-core](https://github.com/openreachtech/hora-skills-ort-core/tree/HEAD/kit/skills/hoc-jest) · ★17)
- **hoc-jsdoc**: JSDoc writing conventions shared by backend and frontend. Use when writing or reviewing JSDoc, the type a tag carries included, in plain JavaScript or in Vue. Use it also when clearing a type error a checker such as `tsc` reports: the error is cleared by writing the annotation, and which annotations may be written is decided here. Reading what the checker reported belongs to the type-errors convention. ([openreachtech/hora-skills-ort-core](https://github.com/openreachtech/hora-skills-ort-core/tree/HEAD/kit/skills/hoc-jsdoc) · ★17)
- **hoc-license**: The `./LICENSE` file a project carries, settled by the `license` field of `package.json`. Use whenever a LICENSE file is created, updated or deleted, or when `license` is changed or found missing. What the README's license section says belongs to the README convention. ([openreachtech/hora-skills-ort-core](https://github.com/openreachtech/hora-skills-ort-core/tree/HEAD/kit/skills/hoc-license) · ★17)
- **hoc-manifest-pattern**: The manifest pattern, also called the super strategy pattern: a structure keeps one shared object declaring its wiring, and everything the structure is made of may take that object. Use when deciding what a class receives, or when a collector starts naming each member's own parameters. The shape of a method's arguments in general belongs to the methods convention. ([openreachtech/hora-skills-ort-core](https://github.com/openreachtech/hora-skills-ort-core/tree/HEAD/kit/skills/hoc-manifest-pattern) · ★17)
- **hoc-may**: Học máy (Learning / Machine Learning) — nghiên cứu và vận dụng học có giám sát, không giám sát, tăng cường, chuyển giao, học sâu, lý thuyết học tính toán và xử lý ngôn ngữ tự nhiên (NLP). Dùng khi cần xác định bài toán học từ dữ liệu, huấn luyện/đánh giá mô hình, phân loại, hồi quy, tìm mẫu, xây pipeline hoặc đánh giá GPT/Transformer. Kết hợp Skill Trí tuệ nhân tạo và Đấu Tranh Nội Tâm. ([2708halinh-cloud/SOL-LONG-MACH](https://github.com/2708halinh-cloud/SOL-LONG-MACH/tree/HEAD/CHATGPT_LIBRARY/SOL%20LONG%20M%E1%BA%A0CH%20-%20H%E1%BA%ACU%20THI%C3%8AN%20T%C4%A8NH/OS_WORK/tools/_Skills/hoc-may) · ★3)
- **hoc-methods**: Conventions for class method definitions and their signatures. Use when defining, calling or reviewing a method. Method names belong to the naming convention; what the body is written with, to the statements convention; how a factory method builds a dependency, to the dependency-wiring convention; how a recursion is laid out, to the method recursion convention. ([openreachtech/hora-skills-ort-core](https://github.com/openreachtech/hora-skills-ort-core/tree/HEAD/kit/skills/hoc-methods) · ★17)
- **hoc-methods-recursion**: How a recursive method is laid out: the entry point callers reach, the member that recurses behind it, and where the state a recursion carries from call to call is started. Use when writing or reviewing a method that calls itself, or one that walks a tree or another nested structure. The prefix the recursing member wears belongs to the method super-prefix convention; method signatures in general, to the method-definition convention. ([openreachtech/hora-skills-ort-core](https://github.com/openreachtech/hora-skills-ort-core/tree/HEAD/kit/skills/hoc-methods-recursion) · ★17)
- **hoc-modules-exports**: Conventions for module exports. Don't define files that merely named-export a function; define a class per responsibility. ([openreachtech/hora-skills-ort-core](https://github.com/openreachtech/hora-skills-ort-core/tree/HEAD/kit/skills/hoc-modules-exports) · ★17)
- **hoc-modules-imports**: Conventions for module imports. Group imports at the top of the file, ordered from farthest to nearest to application development. ([openreachtech/hora-skills-ort-core](https://github.com/openreachtech/hora-skills-ort-core/tree/HEAD/kit/skills/hoc-modules-imports) · ★17)
- **hoc-naming**: Naming conventions for the identifiers in code — classes, members, variables and parameters. Use when choosing or reviewing a name. The super-prefix a method name carries belongs to the method super-prefix convention; constants, to the constants convention; a skill's name, to the skill-updating convention; commit verbs, to the git commit convention. ([openreachtech/hora-skills-ort-core](https://github.com/openreachtech/hora-skills-ort-core/tree/HEAD/kit/skills/hoc-naming) · ★17)
- **hoc-notation**: How a text that names several things of one kind is written: never as an enumeration, whatever the connector. Use when writing or reviewing any text that names more than one thing, or when no single name for the whole can be found. Which language a text is written in belongs to the writing-language convention; what a document states, to the documentation convention. ([openreachtech/hora-skills-ort-core](https://github.com/openreachtech/hora-skills-ort-core/tree/HEAD/kit/skills/hoc-notation) · ★17)
- **hoc-npm-adopt-tool**: Whether a third-party tool may be brought into a project at all. Use when a package is proposed for a problem the project could close by other means, and before anybody installs one the project does not yet carry. What happens once it is in belongs to the dependency conventions beside this one. ([openreachtech/hora-skills-ort-core](https://github.com/openreachtech/hora-skills-ort-core/tree/HEAD/kit/skills/hoc-npm-adopt-tool) · ★17)
- **hoc-npm-categorize-deps**: Which field of the manifest a package is declared in. Use when adding a package to a manifest, and whenever a published package has to say what it needs from whoever installs it. Moving a version already declared belongs to the dependency-raising convention. ([openreachtech/hora-skills-ort-core](https://github.com/openreachtech/hora-skills-ort-core/tree/HEAD/kit/skills/hoc-npm-categorize-deps) · ★17)
- **hoc-npm-install**: How a package is added to a project with `npm install`: what is settled before the command runs, and what to do when it stops. Use before running `npm install &lt;package&gt;`, and when it ends in an error such as `ENOVERSIONS` or leaves something it did not complete. Whether to adopt the package, which manifest field holds it, and what release age, install scripts or advisories require belong to the adopt-tool, categorize, vulnerability and install-scripts conventions. ([openreachtech/hora-skills-ort-core](https://github.com/openreachtech/hora-skills-ort-core/tree/HEAD/kit/skills/hoc-npm-install) · ★17)
- **hoc-npm-install-scripts**: How to handle the install scripts npm would run while installing dependencies. Use this skill when an install reports scripts not yet covered by `allowScripts`, when deciding whether a dependency's install script may be denied, when turning the gate on in a project, or when judging whether an upgrade introduces a new script. Advisories belong to the vulnerability convention; how `.npmrc` is written, to the npmrc convention. ([openreachtech/hora-skills-ort-core](https://github.com/openreachtech/hora-skills-ort-core/tree/HEAD/kit/skills/hoc-npm-install-scripts) · ★17)
- **hoc-npm-npmrc**: How the npm configuration file `.npmrc` is written: each setting as `key = value`, with spaces on both sides of the `=`, edited directly and never through `npm config set`. Use when adding, changing or reviewing a line of `.npmrc`, or when about to run `npm config set`. What each setting does belongs to the convention that turns it on — the install-scripts gate, the release-age quarantine. ([openreachtech/hora-skills-ort-core](https://github.com/openreachtech/hora-skills-ort-core/tree/HEAD/kit/skills/hoc-npm-npmrc) · ★17)
- **hoc-npm-publish**: How a package release orders the commits it makes about itself. Use this skill when preparing a release, bumping a package's own version, or when work lands after the bump. Reading the tarball before it goes out belongs to the publish audit convention; moving the dependency versions a release takes in, to the dependency-raising convention. ([openreachtech/hora-skills-ort-core](https://github.com/openreachtech/hora-skills-ort-core/tree/HEAD/kit/skills/hoc-npm-publish) · ★17)
- **hoc-npm-publish-audit**: The last audit of a package before it reaches the npm registry: it reads what is in the tarball. Use when a package is about to go out, or when asked whether one is fit to. The order of the commits a release makes about itself belongs to the npm publish convention. ([openreachtech/hora-skills-ort-core](https://github.com/openreachtech/hora-skills-ort-core/tree/HEAD/kit/skills/hoc-npm-publish-audit) · ★17)
- **hoc-npm-raise-deps**: How a project's declared dependency versions are raised to the newest release each declared range already permits, including one whose new release has moved to another registry. A major a range excludes is a decision of its own and not this pass. Use when raising dependency versions or comparing them against the registry. Resolving an advisory belongs to the vulnerability convention, and an install script to the install-scripts convention. ([openreachtech/hora-skills-ort-core](https://github.com/openreachtech/hora-skills-ort-core/tree/HEAD/kit/skills/hoc-npm-raise-deps) · ★17)
- **hoc-npm-vulnerability**: How to settle what `npm audit` reports, and how the audit and the release-age quarantine together keep a vulnerable version out of a project. Use when an audit reports an advisory, when a fix is younger than the quarantine, or when no release fixes it. Raising declared versions belongs to the dependency-raising convention; bypassing the quarantine for one install, to the install convention; install scripts, to the install-scripts convention; `.npmrc`, to the npmrc convention. ([openreachtech/hora-skills-ort-core](https://github.com/openreachtech/hora-skills-ort-core/tree/HEAD/kit/skills/hoc-npm-vulnerability) · ★17)
- **hoc-pattern** ([iDorgham/Workspace-Factory](https://github.com/iDorgham/Workspace-Factory/tree/HEAD/factory/library/archive/legacy_pillars/30_web_platforms/02_web_platforms/web_developing/skills/react-patterns/hoc-pattern) · ★2)
- **hoc-pattern**: Use when reading or modernizing a Higher-Order Component (HOC) in legacy code, OR when comparing HOC vs custom hook vs render props for a NEW cross-cutting concern (auth wrap, analytics, error-boundary). In modern React, hooks are usually the right choice — this skill covers the HOC pattern so you can recognize, refactor, or deliberately keep one. NOT for custom hooks (`hooks-pattern`), provider-based dependency injection (`provider-pattern`), or component composition (`react-composition-2026`). ([TierOne-Studio/spa-velocity](https://github.com/TierOne-Studio/spa-velocity/tree/HEAD/.ruler/skills/hoc-pattern) · ★1)
- **hoc-pattern**: Teaches the Higher-Order Component (HOC) pattern for logic reuse. Use when you need to share cross-cutting concerns like authentication, logging, or data fetching across multiple components. ([patternsdev/skills](https://github.com/patternsdev/skills) · 1.1k installs)
- **hoc-pattern**: Teaches the Higher-Order Component (HOC) pattern for logic reuse. Use when you need to share cross-cutting concerns like authentication, logging, or data fetching across multiple components. ([quanngynx/servexa-warranty-ai](https://github.com/quanngynx/servexa-warranty-ai/tree/HEAD/.agents/skills/hoc-pattern) · 1.1k installs · 11 copies)
- **hoc-prohibit-native-features**: The native JavaScript features this library does not use, and why each is refused. Use as the criterion whenever code is about to reach for a native feature — `Map`, `Object.freeze()`, a `#` private member, a decorator, an object literal handed across scopes — or when reviewing code that does. A class field is refused by the class design principles, which define what a property is. ([openreachtech/hora-skills-ort-core](https://github.com/openreachtech/hora-skills-ort-core/tree/HEAD/kit/skills/hoc-prohibit-native-features) · ★17)
- **hoc-properties**: Conventions for class properties — where they are set, whether they may change, and what may hold them. Use when declaring, assigning or reviewing a property of a class, including when a note writes a member as `#alpha`. `Map` and native private belong to the native-feature prohibition convention; accessors, to the accessors convention; whether a class needs properties at all, to the class design principles convention. ([openreachtech/hora-skills-ort-core](https://github.com/openreachtech/hora-skills-ort-core/tree/HEAD/kit/skills/hoc-properties) · ★17)
- **hoc-readme**: The README a project carries, in every language it keeps, and the parts of it split out under `docs/`. Use whenever a README is created or updated. Which `LICENSE` file the project carries belongs to the license convention, and what a document may state as fact to the documentation convention. ([openreachtech/hora-skills-ort-core](https://github.com/openreachtech/hora-skills-ort-core/tree/HEAD/kit/skills/hoc-readme) · ★17)
- **hoc-requirement-definition**: Turn a rough, incomplete request into a requirement definition document through conversation with the requester. Use this skill whenever the user brings a feature request, ticket or rough idea and asks to define or write up the requirements, and before implementing anything whose scope is not already written down. Tracking progress against the requirements belongs to the implementation- progress convention. ([openreachtech/hora-skills-ort-core](https://github.com/openreachtech/hora-skills-ort-core/tree/HEAD/kit/skills/hoc-requirement-definition) · ★17)
- **hoc-resolve-shorthand**: Resolve a shorthand where the reader meets it, so that following it costs no lookup. Use when anything written for a reader points at an issue, a phase, a rule or a list entry by a number or a label instead of by what it is. The `#instanceMember` notation belongs to the member notation convention. ([openreachtech/hora-skills-ort-core](https://github.com/openreachtech/hora-skills-ort-core/tree/HEAD/kit/skills/hoc-resolve-shorthand) · ★17)
- **hoc-retake-declaration**: Conventions for redoing existing code without moving what its callers see, and for telling a retake from an update. Use before reshaping a class or a member that already has callers. The release that would admit a moved interface belongs to the npm publish convention. ([openreachtech/hora-skills-ort-core](https://github.com/openreachtech/hora-skills-ort-core/tree/HEAD/kit/skills/hoc-retake-declaration) · ★17)
- **hoc-scope**: Conventions for how class members refer to one another across the static and instance sides. Use when a member refers to another member, to its own class, or to a property of `this`. How an instance reaches its own class through `#get:Ctor` belongs to the Ctor convention; what a class may hold, to the class design principles convention; how a getter is written, to the accessors convention. ([openreachtech/hora-skills-ort-core](https://github.com/openreachtech/hora-skills-ort-core/tree/HEAD/kit/skills/hoc-scope) · ★17)
- **hoc-sinh-ao**: Học sinh ảo — đọc kịch bản (script.json) hoặc storyboard của một video giải thích như một học sinh THPT trung bình xem lần đầu, báo mọi chỗ bị lạc, nhảy bước, không biết đang nhìn gì, nghe khó. Dùng ở skill viet-kich-ban Bước 3 và dung-video Bước 2b. Người gọi phải cho biết tiền đề (học sinh đã học gì). ([PhcdY/remotion_claudecode](https://github.com/PhcdY/remotion_claudecode/tree/HEAD/.agents/skills/hoc-sinh-ao))
- **hoc-skill-updating**: Conventions for creating new skills (`SKILL.md`) or updating existing ones, in any of the libraries. Use before naming, placing, writing or splitting a skill, or editing its description. How prose is written in general belongs to the documentation convention. ([openreachtech/hora-skills-ort-core](https://github.com/openreachtech/hora-skills-ort-core/tree/HEAD/kit/skills/hoc-skill-updating) · ★17)
- **hoc-sprite-animation**: Create or transfer active Heroes of Crypto creature sprite animations from a source creature image and a Sprite AI 256x256 motion draft, then redraw identity-preserving high-resolution frames, validate motion, and package atlas/meta files. Use for walking, attacks, reactions, death, or other active motion transfer; do not use for standing idle loops, breathing, hover-in-place, or static portraits. ([o1dstaRs/heroes-of-crypto-client](https://github.com/o1dstaRs/heroes-of-crypto-client/tree/HEAD/.claude/skills/hoc-sprite-animation) · ★3)
- **hoc-statements**: Conventions for statements and control flow inside a function or method body, test files included. Use when writing or reviewing any body of code, a destructuring or a method read out of its receiver included. How a higher-order function is used belongs to the higher-order function convention; where an expression is wrapped across lines, to the coding style convention; a method's signature, to the methods convention. ([openreachtech/hora-skills-ort-core](https://github.com/openreachtech/hora-skills-ort-core/tree/HEAD/kit/skills/hoc-statements) · ★17)
- **hoc-super-prefix-method-name**: The super-prefixes a method name may carry before its verb, and what each one promises about how the method behaves. Use when naming or reviewing a method that handles many items at once, realizes a recursion, or calls a delegate. The rest of a method name belongs to the naming convention; how a recursion is laid out, to the method recursion convention; how the call to a delegate is isolated, to the delegation convention. ([openreachtech/hora-skills-ort-core](https://github.com/openreachtech/hora-skills-ort-core/tree/HEAD/kit/skills/hoc-super-prefix-method-name) · ★17)
- **hoc-test-cache**: Reuse a recorded test pass when a verification unit's inputs are unchanged, through the `mentsu-testcase-cache` CLI. Use when wiring cached test execution into a repository, or when reading a cached run's verdict. Driving a failing suite to green belongs to the test-execution convention; recording the verdict belongs to the acceptance run. ([openreachtech/hora-skills-ort-core](https://github.com/openreachtech/hora-skills-ort-core/tree/HEAD/kit/skills/hoc-test-cache) · ★17)
- **hoc-test-execution**: Run a project's test cases and drive them to green without weakening them — no test skipped, deleted or loosened to make the suite pass. Use this skill whenever tests are run, whenever a test still fails after an implementation was believed finished, and before claiming an implementation is complete. Writing new tests belongs to the project's test-writing convention; reusing a recorded pass over unchanged inputs, to the test-cache convention. ([openreachtech/hora-skills-ort-core](https://github.com/openreachtech/hora-skills-ort-core/tree/HEAD/kit/skills/hoc-test-execution) · ★17)
- **hoc-type-errors**: How to read what a type checker reports, before deciding what to change. Use when a `tsc` or editor diagnostic is being cleared. Which annotation to write, and which casts are refused, belong to the JSDoc convention; what a test's fixtures hold belongs to the Jest convention. ([openreachtech/hora-skills-ort-core](https://github.com/openreachtech/hora-skills-ort-core/tree/HEAD/kit/skills/hoc-type-errors) · ★17)
- **hoc-vscode-cspell**: Conventions for the spell checker's vocabulary in `.vscode/cspell.json`, and for settling the words it reports. Use when cspell flags a word, when adding to or pruning `words:` or `ignoreWords:`, or when tempted to reword code to silence it. How names are spelled in the first place belongs to the naming convention; a lint rule that fires belongs to the ESLint conventions. ([openreachtech/hora-skills-ort-core](https://github.com/openreachtech/hora-skills-ort-core/tree/HEAD/kit/skills/hoc-vscode-cspell) · ★17)
- **hoc-wire-dependencies**: How a class reaches the modules and classes it depends on: never by the name it imported, always through a static getter, so that a subclass can patch one and a test can swap it. Use when a class imports anything it uses, a native module such as `fs` included, builds a dependency in `static create()` or anywhere else, or a dependency has to be patched or mocked. How a getter's body is written belongs to the accessors convention; `static create()` in general, to the methods convention. ([openreachtech/hora-skills-ort-core](https://github.com/openreachtech/hora-skills-ort-core/tree/HEAD/kit/skills/hoc-wire-dependencies) · ★17)
- **hoc-workflows**: Development workflow conventions for carrying an implementation from its start to its completion. Use when starting implementation work, when the surrounding code disagrees with a convention, before committing, and before declaring the work complete. What a commit holds and how it is worded belongs to the git commit convention. ([openreachtech/hora-skills-ort-core](https://github.com/openreachtech/hora-skills-ort-core/tree/HEAD/kit/skills/hoc-workflows) · ★17)
- **hoc-writing-language**: Which natural language a piece of writing is in — code, comments, a code example, a skill, a README, a document written for a reader — and the order that resolves it: an explicit instruction, then the language the artifact names for itself, then the language an existing document is already in, then the default for its kind. Use when about to write any of them, or when the language of one is in question. Which language files a README keeps belongs to the README convention. ([openreachtech/hora-skills-ort-core](https://github.com/openreachtech/hora-skills-ort-core/tree/HEAD/kit/skills/hoc-writing-language) · ★17)

Write-capable; requires --confirm. Emits MCP command objects. ([aibtcdev/skills](https://github.com/aibtcdev/skills/tree/HEAD/hodlmm-arb-executor) · ★11)
- **hodlmm-bin-guardian**: Monitors Bitflow HODLMM bins to keep LP positions in the active earning range. Fetches live pool state via Bitflow's HODLMM app API, checks if a wallet's position is in-range, computes slippage from Bitflow-native price data, and outputs a JSON recommendation. Read-only — rebalance actions require explicit human approval. ([aibtcdev/skills](https://github.com/aibtcdev/skills/tree/HEAD/hodlmm-bin-guardian) · ★11 · 2 copies)
- **hodlmm-emergency-exit**: Autonomous capital protection for HODLMM LP positions. Composes sbtc-proof-of-reserve (peg safety) and bin-range analysis into a single exit decision engine. When sBTC reserve is RED or bins drift out of range beyond the grace period, outputs executable MCP withdrawal commands to remove all liquidity. The defensive counterpart to HODLMM yield strategies. ([cliqueengagements/30-Days-AI-Challenge](https://github.com/cliqueengagements/30-Days-AI-Challenge/tree/HEAD/day-5-hodlmm-emergency-exit) · ★2)
- **hodlmm-flow**: Swap flow intelligence for Bitflow HODLMM — analyzes on-chain swap transactions to compute direction bias, flow toxicity, bin velocity, whale concentration, and bot/organic classification for LP decision-making. ([aibtcdev/skills](https://github.com/aibtcdev/skills/tree/HEAD/hodlmm-flow) · ★11)
- **hodlmm-inventory-balancer**: Detects HODLMM LP inventory drift (token-ratio imbalance from one-sided swap flow) and restores the target ratio via a corrective Bitflow swap plus a hodlmm-move-liquidity redeploy, gated by the 4h per-pool cooldown. ([aibtcdev/skills](https://github.com/aibtcdev/skills/tree/HEAD/hodlmm-inventory-balancer) · ★11 · 2 copies)
- **hodlmm-move-liquidity**: HODLMM Move-Liquidity & Auto-Rebalancer — withdraw from drifted bins, re-deposit around the current active bin. Includes autonomous monitoring loop. ([aibtcdev/skills](https://github.com/aibtcdev/skills/tree/HEAD/hodlmm-move-liquidity) · ★11 · 3 copies)
- **hodlmm-position-exit**: Pure-exit skill for HODLMM concentrated-liquidity positions on Bitflow. Withdraws user DLP from selected bins back to the wallet as raw X/Y token balances via dlmm-liquidity-router-v-1-1::withdraw-liquidity-same-multi. No rebalance, no redeploy, no cross-protocol rotation. Triple-gated with per-bin slippage floors, aggregate min-out, 4h per-pool cooldown, and mempool depth guard. Password prompted interactively — no env var, no CLI flag. ([cliqueengagements/30-Days-AI-Challenge](https://github.com/cliqueengagements/30-Days-AI-Challenge/tree/HEAD/day-26-hodlmm-position-exit) · ★2)
